# Axis M: Security, tests and deployment

Detail cards of the master tracker ([`MASTER_IMPLEMENTATION_TRACKER.md`](../MASTER_IMPLEMENTATION_TRACKER.md)), as of 2026-11-23. Requirements: 63. Acceptance criteria: 311 (MET 106, CODED 118, MISSING 87). Code completion 72%, proven by a passing test 34%, release readiness 3%.

Independent verification: done for unit(s) M1, M2, M3.

Every card gives the ten fields of the directive: (1) id and name, (2) functional requirement, (3) what exists in main, (4) what the draft pull requests did, (5) what remains, (6) pull requests and commits, (7) tests that exist and where they ran, (8) dependencies and blockers, (9) acceptance criteria marked MET (a named passing test or CI job on a named head), CODED (code exists, no passing test cited) or MISSING, (10) the next coding task.

  - [MET] Each of the five resolutions is pinned by a test, and each pin was checked by undoing the resolution it pins:
    - **`app/Utils/module-helper.php`**, both halves at once -- the GCC chain's `PaymentStorefront::within` wrapper and the security set's settlement scope. `StorefrontPaymentCallbackTest::test_two_payments_of_two_markets_settled_one_after_the_other_each_build_their_own` settles a Kuwaiti and an Emirati payment one after the other in one process, each payer holding a row of both markets, and each order takes the row of the market its payment was started in: take the wrapper out and it fails. `test_a_second_report_of_one_payment_builds_no_second_order_even_with_a_cart_to_build_from` refills the cart before the gateway reports again, which is the state the scope exists for (a clearing that failed, a recovery sweep): take the scope check out and a second order is built.
    - **`RestAPI/v1/CartController`**, the guest-and-owner rule: `tests/Feature/Cart/CartRowOwnershipTest.php` (10 cases), including the API select action and the two cases where a guest and a customer share a number.
    - **`RestAPI/v2/seller/ProductController` and `RestAPI/v3/seller/ProductController`**, the code lookup: `tests/Feature/Catalog/ProductCodeLookupGuardTest.php`, whose rule is that every lookup by SKU, barcode or variant SKU is either routed through `ProductCodeScope` or listed in its table with a reason -- so a resolution that stopped routing one of those two files fails it by construction, and the registry is exact (a count that differs fails too).
    - **`CartManager`**: `StorefrontCartGuardTest`, `StorefrontCartIsolationTest` and `StorefrontCartEdgesTest` for the storefront half, `CartRowOwnershipTest` for the ownership half.
- **(10) Next coding task.** None. The integration branches this requirement was written around are history: every set is in `main`, `main`'s own CI is green, and the five resolutions are pinned. #### M1-04. Stage 0: open maintenance routes removed and one-time codes kept out of the log

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines directive (axis M) | SECURITY_RELEASE_PLAN stage 0 and decision 15; DEFERRED_RISKS DR-1 (no rotation) | reviews: security, owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** No route file can be triggered by a web request to run commands or write files, and no log call records a one-time code, a phone paired with a code, or the text of a message that carries one.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** origin/main removed the three maintenance routes (b6d35ce7, with MaintenanceRoutesTest). main, PR 75 and PR 83 still log the vendor reset's phone and code at five points (ForgotPasswordController) and the SMS request text ('message' => $message in ZenderOtpService).
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** PR 85 (b83fd18, cut from the baseline, not for main): removes the routes (85 lines) and the log calls, hides the WhatsApp message, adds MaintenanceRoutesTest and OtpLoggingTest; it is the 26th commit of the hotfix tip fafa1a2e. CI of PR 85: 7 of 7 jobs green, overlay included.
- **(5) What remains.** (1) Port the log removal and OtpLoggingTest to main: no main-cut PR carries it. (2) Decide what happens to codes already written to storage/logs. (3) Read CI of the hotfix tip fafa1a2e.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#85](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/85) `b83fd18`; [lavkoo#78](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/78) `665057a`; [lavkoo#106](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/106) `a8069b1a`
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `ci` MaintenanceRoutesTest (2 tests) and OtpLoggingTest (3 tests) (PR 85 b83fd18: SQLite PHP 8.2/8.3 and Fulfillment + security MySQL 8 green (7 of 7 jobs))
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** Decision 2 (route A or B): on route A the log removal must exist on main. Old log files are the administrator's and the owner's decision.
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [MET] No route file registers a route that runs commands, changes permissions, writes files or holds a key (MaintenanceRoutesTest on main and on PR 85 b83fd18, green)
  - [CODED] The vendor password reset writes no code and no phone to the log (baseline tree) (OtpLoggingTest PR 85 b83fd18, SQLite and MySQL 8)
  - [MET] The SMS and WhatsApp gateway log neither the message nor a code (baseline tree) (OtpLoggingTest PR 85 b83fd18)
  - [MET] The same holds on main, so a main-based release also keeps codes out of the log (PR 106 (draft, main based, a8069b1a): the five OTP log calls of the vendor password reset and the message of the Zender gateway are removed from main (the same change as stage 0 of the hotfix, PR 85); OtpLoggingTest scans every Log:: call of the two files and fails for a code, a token or a message that is logged; the gateway still logs the phone number and its own answer, which is the owner's call. CI run 342 (the all-in-one integration, head a5f7de59, which holds this pull request's code), read per job on 2026-11-18: 'Tests, SQLite' on PHP 8.2 and on PHP 8.3 (the whole suite), the Storefront, Address, Catalog, Staff scope, Checkout/payment/permission, Fulfillment + security, Saudi baseline and Wallet/Loyalty groups on MySQL 8.0 and on MariaDB 11.4.2 and the strict-mode job on MariaDB, and the five sales-channel and cost groups on MySQL 8.0 and MariaDB 11.4.2 (the last to finish, group A to M on MySQL 8.0, at 16:46 UTC): success (32 jobs: 30 success, 2 skipped by design, the deployment overlay and the Saudi baseline expectations check). A job proves a test only when its path list holds the directory of that test: the strict-mode job's list holds neither tests/Feature/Storefront nor tests/Feature/Address, so what runs in those directories has run under strict mode only locally. The pull request's own run is not read)
- **(10) Next coding task.** Cut a branch from main: apply PR 85's ForgotPasswordController and ZenderOtpService hunks (the routes are already gone), add OtpLoggingTest, run Fulfillment + security on MySQL 8 and MariaDB 11.4.2 and open it as a draft.

#### M1-05. Stage 1: account takeover closed for customer, delivery man, seller, website and app

- **State: IMPLEMENTED IN DRAFT** | plan lines directive (axis M) | SECURITY_RELEASE_PLAN stage 1 and decisions 1, 3, 4; OWNER_PR_GUIDE section 1; customer-app audit 2026-11-17 | reviews: security, owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** Knowing only a phone number or e-mail is not enough to reset a password, sign in, confirm a social login or read a seller's credentials: resets name one account by equality with a proof only the server issues, public seller registration cannot approve itself, and codes are random when APP_MODE=live.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** origin/main has none of the eight fixes. config/app.php defines 'mode' (APP_MODE, default live) but no test pins it; the server baseline lacks that line. The customer app's main has no CI workflow.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** PRs 62, 58, 59, 45, 60, 44, 61, 57 with tests, carried in the PR 75 tree and ported as eight commits plus the config commit 779a3e50 on the hotfix; three data-only migrations purge reset codes in flight and clear seller remember-me tokens; lavkooapp#4 sends the server's temporary_token with the existing-account answer.

CI run 367 (pull_request, head ce8786b7): 'Fulfillment + security tests', which runs tests/Feature/Security, green on MySQL 8 (1641 tests, 8547 assertions, 3 skipped, all three the PilotServer engine skips) and on MariaDB 11.4.2 (green in the run's job list; its log is not kept), and the whole suite on SQLite 8.2 and 8.3 (6268 tests, 97 skipped, 1 failure, TorodShipmentDiscoveryTest, which is red on main); the 97 skips are listed in both SQLite logs and none is of this class. The strict-mode job does not run tests/Feature/Security. Asserted: config/app.php's mode is the APP_MODE line of the environment and live when there is none (4 data sets, the file re-required with APP_MODE written to and removed from $_ENV, $_SERVER and the process environment); with app.mode set to live, three repeated requests to each of nine real routes (customer check-phone, check-email, forgot-password and resend-otp-check-phone; delivery-man v2, seller v3 and seller v2 forgot-password; the website's resend-otp-code; the vendor panel's forgot-password) and to CustomerAuthService::getCustomerVerificationToken give a code of the documented six or four digits that is never the fixed test code and not the same for all three; in the mode that keeps the fixed code every request gets it, and the older seller app keeps 1234 in dev mode only; two scans of app/ put every literal 123456 or 1234 at one of the twelve known mode deciders with its condition, random form and fixed value, and find no four-to-eight-digit number assigned to a $token, $otp or $code. Audit mutations on that head: the APP_MODE default changed from live to demo turns the 'no APP_MODE line' case red; a constant 654321 of the right shape at check-phone turns that route's live case and the first scan red; the vendor panel decider inverted so live gets 123456 turns that route's live case, its fixed-mode case and the first scan red. Not covered: 'every one-time code' is not proven. Ten of the twelve mode deciders are driven; the two that no route reaches (RestAPI\v1\auth\ForgotPasswordController::reset_password_request and PhoneVerificationController::check_phone) rest on the two scans alone, as a fixed code written there turns only the scans red. The routed code issuers that never consult the mode have no behaviour test at all - resend-otp-check-email in EmailVerificationController, the website's phone and e-mail verification in WebController, the delivery-man profile code and the order verification codes - and a constant written at the e-mail resend in a form the scans do not match ((int) str_pad('9', 4, '9')) keeps all 27 tests green. The scans read app/ only, not Modules/. The live cases set config('app.mode') directly rather than the environment variable, so the APP_MODE-to-code chain is closed by the config test and not end to end. 'Random' is asserted as differing between requests and never the fixed code, not as unpredictable: the tests seed mt_srand so rand stays reproducible. And main's website resend for a customer with no stored code still ends in a 500 at new PhoneOrEmailVerification() in a file that imports only the repository interface, which the test works around and does not pin.)

#### M1-11. Deployment tooling: overlay build, drift check, apply, backup and rollback, and its CI job

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines directive (axis M); 560-562 | SECURITY_RELEASE_PLAN sections 4 and 5 and appendix B; OWNER_PR_GUIDE section 6; decision 14 | reviews: security, owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** A release is built as an overlay of added and modified files under app, bootstrap, config, database, resources and routes, checked against the server's files (CLEAN, MERGE, NEW, DRIFTED) before anything is written, applied with backups outside the tree, taken back by rollback, and refused by name when it needs a deletion, a link or a file outside those directories.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** origin/main and the baseline carry scripts/deploy (build-overlay.php, deploy.php with verify, apply and rollback, backup.sh, verify-backup.sh) and 108 test methods in tests/Unit/Deploy. The CI job 'Deployment overlay (artifact, deploys nothing)' builds the package from PILOT_BASELINE (default 698aa1e) on every PR; the tool refuses what it cannot carry ('Deploy it by hand, deliberately').
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** PR 85 (stage 0, 3 files): overlay job green. PRs 78, 81, 86 (hotfix tree): job red on purpose, 17 files lie outside the overlay directories; the release uses a package commit that restores them and a shell loop with checksums. PR 75: red for 20 files.
- **(5) What remains.** (1) The 17 files have no tool: no drift check, apply or rollback in deploy.php, only sha256 lists and a loop in the runbook. (2) An overlay never deletes (PR 3 deletes two files). (3) The job stays red on every main-cut branch (three files; 395 commits). (4) PILOT_BASELINE is unreadable here. (5) Package hashes hold only until a branch is rebuilt.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#85](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/85) `b83fd18`; [lavkoo#78](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/78) `665057a`; [lavkoo#81](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/81) `e1f083c`; [lavkoo#86](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/86) `57900cc`; [lavkoo#75](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/75) `47b78ab`
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `ci` OverlayDeploymentTest (65 methods), BackupScriptsTest (22), LeakGuardTest (21) (origin/main tests/Unit/Deploy; whole suite on SQLite PHP 8.2/8.3 plus the root re-run of tests/Unit/Deploy, green on every head including PR 85 b83fd18)
  - `ci` Deployment overlay job builds the 3-file package of stage 0 (PR 85 b83fd18: job green)
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** Decision 14: the red job is expected and is not bypassed; neither the job nor the deploy scripts may be changed to hide the 17 files.
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [MET] An overlay holds exactly the added and modified files that run on the server, with the baseline checksum of each (test_the_overlay_holds_exactly_the_added_and_modified_files_that_run_on_the_server, main)
  - [MET] A release needing a deletion, a link, a mode change or a file outside the six directories is refused by name (test_a_release_an_overlay_cannot_deploy_is_refused_by_name, main)
  - [MET] A drifted file stops the deployment and changes nothing; language files are merged, never overwritten (drift test and test_language_files_are_merged_never_overwritten, main)
  - [MET] A plain apply is a dry run; a real apply backs up outside the tree; a damaged backup is detected (test_a_plain_apply_is_a_dry_run; ...backs_up_outside_the_tree; BackupScriptsTest damaged-backup test)
  - [MET] A rollback restores every file exactly, a second one changes nothing, it will not destroy a file changed after the deployment, and a cut-off deployment takes itself back (OverlayDeploymentTest rollback, cut-off and full-disk tests, main)
  - [MISSING] Files outside the overlay directories are checked, applied and rolled back by the tool (17 files go by a shell loop with sha256 lists (runbook H-1, H-4, section 5))
- **(10) Next coding task.** Add a by-hand set to scripts/deploy: a manifest of files outside the six directories with baseline and new checksums, verify (CLEAN or DRIFTED), apply with backups and rollback, tested in tests/Unit/Deploy/OverlayDeploymentTest; keep the refusal by default and leave the CI overlay job unchanged.
- **Open owner decisions.** 14 (the overlay job stays red); set the repository variable PILOT_BASELINE after the first deployment; whether to extend the tool or keep the manual loop.

#### M1-16. Protection of main and CI cost and reliability

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 164, 562 | DEFERRED_RISKS DR-3, DR-4, DR-5; charter section 4 (branch protection not authorized); handoff 2026-11-17 CI section | reviews: owner-decision

#### M1-18. Engineering safety rules: classify, preserve, extend, isolate, verify; no direct SQL; no weakened protection

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 539, 546-562 | charter sections 3 to 5; Discovery-01 reports 04 and 07; DECISIONS guardrails 1 to 6; OWNER_PR_GUIDE section 4 (kill switch) | reviews: owner-decision, security
- **(2) Functional requirement.** Each feature is classified before it is designed, no working engine is rewritten, new tables and changes to historical relations carry a compatibility and rollback plan, production changes use no direct SQL or quick fix, no protection (route gates, leak guard, version gate, tests) is weakened, and every new capability can be switched off again.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** Discovery-01 reports 04 (a five-class matrix: Preserve, Extend, Refactor, New, Dependent-On-PR) and 07 (additive-only migration and rollback plan) are on the docs branch. main runs the leak guard, AdminRouteAuthenticationTest and the deploy tests on every PR. The runbook allows only by-path data migrations and read-only SQL.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** PR 83: every GCC table is added by migrations whose tests apply and roll back (PlatformAdmin MigrationsTest, GrantsMigrationTest, StaffCountryMigrationTest); StorefrontKillSwitchTest pins that deactivating a country is the kill switch. PR 67: AdminRouteTableTest fails a new unguarded admin route.

(2) The classification lacks 'Reuse As-Is' and 'Unknown / Requires Verification' as classes. (3) AdminRouteTableTest exists only in PR 67, not on main.

  - `ci` PlatformAdmin MigrationsTest, GrantsMigrationTest, StaffCountryMigrationTest, StorefrontKillSwitchTest (PR 83 8ff3410: SQLite 8.2/8.3; Storefront and StaffScope groups on MySQL 8 and MariaDB 11.4.2 green)
  - `ci` LeakGuardTest, AdminRouteAuthenticationTest (main); AdminRouteTableTest (PR 67) (green on every head; PR 67 822e16c)
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** Which switches may be turned off again is a product decision; the version-gate test and the overlay job must stay untouched.
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [CODED] Each feature is classified (preserve, extend, refactor, new, dependent on an open PR, unknown) before it is designed (Discovery-01 report 04 uses five classes; 'Reuse As-Is' and 'Unknown' are not separate)

DR-1 defers rotation, not scanning, and the charter allows CI work, so this is open development work.

- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [MET] The backend repository refuses a pull request that adds a secret or personal data (Origin/main (c5c04b7b) .github/workflows/ci.yml lines 152-165 hold the job `leak-guard`, named 'No secret or personal data added', with no condition and a full-history checkout, which runs `php scripts/ci/leak-guard.php --base="${{ github.event.pull_request.base.sha || github.event.before || 'origin/main' }}"`. The workflow starts on every pull request, on a push to main only and on a manual run (lines 5-9), so a pull request is scanned over its own range and a push to main against the commit before it; a push to any other branch starts no run. The job, the scanner (scripts/ci/leak-guard.php) and its test are on all 134 open pull request heads. The test, tests/Unit/Deploy/LeakGuardTest.php (21 methods: exit status 1 on an added secret that is never quoted, a merge commit as head, Saudi mobile numbers and e-mail addresses), is byte-identical on origin/main and on head 8df2153d, where CI run 44566139353 (pull_request, an earlier head of PR 132) passed it: 'Tests, SQLite' on PHP 8.2 and 8.3 are green with 6210 tests and 97 skips, none of them of this class, and the job 'No secret or personal data added' of that run is green. The job has also gone red on a pull request: run 43937162831 (PR 63, head 124d68e3), one finding of rule api-token, exit 1. Two limits. It is a red check and not a gate: GitHub reports main as unprotected with no ruleset, and run 43031304991 (push, 352ed688) failed this job with 409 findings while 352ed688 is on origin/main; the rule that would require the check is a document in draft PR 91 (2a175311), applied nowhere (M1-16, DR-4). Its personal-data rules are an e-mail address and a Saudi mobile number: numbers written +971, +965, +974, +973 or +968 break no rule of origin/main's scanner, which the Discovery records as open (Q-47 and owner decision D-04 on the docs branch).)
  - [MISSING] The SELLER app repository refuses one too (Lavkooappseller origin/main (d556fb4f) .github/workflows/ci.yml holds two jobs, `analyze-and-test` (install, verify formatting of changed Dart files, analyze, check translation keys, test) and `build-android`; searching it for leak, secret, gitleaks or trufflehog returns zero matches. No other ref has a scan either: none of the 21 remote refs, the 17 open pull request heads among them, holds a file that names a secret scanner, and none of the 12 distinct versions of ci.yml on them has a scan step. So no check of the repository's own challenges a committed key, and main is unprotected (GitHub reports protected false and no ruleset). Whether GitHub's own secret scanning or push protection is on for this public repository could not be read from here: the repository API answers security_and_analysis null and the proxy refuses the secret-scanning endpoint.)
  - [MISSING] The CUSTOMER app repository refuses one too (lavkooapp origin/main has NO .github directory at all — `git ls-tree -r --name-only origin/main | grep -c '^.github/'` is 0, so the repository has no CI of any kind on its default branch. Some gcc/* branches carry a workflow; the branch that would have to enforce it does not. This is the weakest of the three and the one a reader would most easily assume was covered by the other two.)
  - [CODED] What happens on detection is written down (SEC-E21-04 step 6 of the triage (docs branch, 01-security-triage-public.md line 79) asks for secret scanning in every repository's release gate together with a definition of the action on detection. For the backend that action is written on origin/main (c5c04b7b): scripts/ci/leak-guard.php ends a run that found something with exit status 1 and the lines 'leak-guard: N finding(s). The matched text is not printed on purpose.' and 'Remove the data and rotate anything that was real: a secret in an earlier commit is still in the history. A false alarm is allowed with a reasoned entry in scripts/ci/leak-guard-allow.json.' (lines 492-495), and docs/integrations/security-findings.md lines 210-216 say the same in prose ('it is in the pushed history and has to be rotated'). LeakGuardTest pins the exit status and that the match is never quoted, not the advice, and no test reads the document. Three things are written nowhere. Who is told when the job goes red: the only line of that kind is line 46 of docs/gcc/PHASE0-B-SAUDI-REGRESSION-CI-AUTHORIZATION.md (docs branch and the head of PR 1), 'stop and alert owner privately without printing it', and it is about a live secret seen in CI logs during that one workstream. How 'rotate anything that was real' is to happen while DEFERRED_RISKS.md DR-1 (docs branch) records 'no rotation, revocation or replacement now' and the charter (origin/main, line 50) keeps the rotation of real credentials for the owner. And anything at all for the seller and customer apps, which have no scan on any ref and therefore no action.)
- **(10) Next coding task.** See the criteria: the unmet ones name the file and the line that has to change.

#### M2-04. Unified customer account across countries (test plan 4)

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 662 | GCC-13; DECISIONS (one customer sees own orders across countries; consents correctly scoped) | reviews: legal, owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** One customer identity (one login, one phone and e-mail key) works in all six storefronts while carts, orders, wallets and financial flows stay separate. A test signs one customer into two storefronts and checks what is shared and what is not.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** Main has one global users table and no market field on it; the API and web order lists return every registered order of the signed-in customer; SaudiPhone reads a number without a country code as Saudi.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** #48 login moves a guest's rows of each country into that customer's cart of that country (API and web tests). #82 customer, delivery-man and vendor screens list only the orders of the employee's countries; people belong to no country. #43 legal acceptance recorder (test-only). #58 and #62 compare phones by digits for any country code in reset and tracking (security set only).
- **(5) What remains.** No test signs one customer in through two storefronts end to end. No canonical phone and e-mail key with one duplicate check across web, API, OTP, social, POS, admin. No mobile formats for AE, KW, QA, BH, OM. No marketing or legal consents by storefront and entity (the recorder is called by nothing). No market label or filter on the customer's order list. Account deletion that keeps ledgers is open.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#43](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/43) `82b6dfa`; [lavkoo#48](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/48) `4a821d3`; [lavkoo#58](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/58) `40020ae`; [lavkoo#62](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/62) `f96cc6c`; [lavkoo#82](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/82) `d1fc5f4`; [lavkoo#83](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/83) `8ff3410`; [lavkoo#96](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/96) `e624e43e`
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `ci` A guest cart of two countries moves into the customer's per-country carts on API and web login (tests/Feature/Storefront/StorefrontCartEdgesTest (SA, AE); PR 83 8ff3410: SQLite 8.2/8.3, Storefront MySQL 8, MariaDB 11.4.2)
  - `ci` Customer, delivery-man and vendor screens list only scoped orders; people are not narrowed (tests/Feature/StaffScope/StaffPeopleScreensTest (SA, AE, KW); PR 83 Staff scope jobs on both engines)
  - `ci` Phone comparison by digits for any country code; a foreign number never equals a Saudi one (PR 62 f96cc6c: tests/Feature/Address/InputNormalisersTest, tests/Feature/Security/CustomerPasswordResetApiTest; Tests SQLite success (security set only))
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** Phone and consent decisions need the legal view per country. #58 and #62 live in the security set (PR 75); no tree combines them with the storefront chain.
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [MET] One users row signs in through any storefront with the same credentials; no per-storefront user is created (PR 96 (draft, stacked on #87, e624e43e): StorefrontCustomerSignInTest (61 tests) signs one customer in through the real customer login of the API (the storefront named by the X-Storefront header; the answer is a token) and of the website (the answer is the session of the customer guard; the storefront is chosen with ?storefront= and the test reads it back from the request's storefront context) from each of the six storefronts and from none, with the same credentials, next to another registered customer who is left untouched and is issued no token. Every answer is a token for the same user id (the claim of the token and the stored token agree; by e-mail and by phone, 14 tokens for the one id) or the session of the same account, no row is created, and after each e-mail sign-in (API and website) the one users row is unchanged but for its updated_at. A wrong password is refused alike through every storefront (the API counts the failed attempts on the one row: those made from SA, KW and no storefront add up and a sign-in from AE clears them; the website refuses alike, and no test asserts whether it counts attempts). An identity nobody registered creates no user (API and website). The registrations of the API through each storefront and through none write one row each that is the same but for the id, e-mail, phone, password, referral code, temporary token and moments, and no column holds the code of the storefront as its value; the referral code (20 upper-case letters or digits) and the temporary token (40 letters or digits) are checked for their format only, so a storefront code inside them would pass. Structural: a text scan finds no migration of the application or of its modules that names the users table and a storefront (the scan is proved against six spellings it must catch and two it must leave alone; a column that the word storefront does not name is not caught). CustomerAcrossStorefrontsTest (8 tests; among them the customer info from each of the six storefronts, the same id and e-mail with no user created, and the order list of six orders from each storefront and from none). #### M2-14. Stopping a store and handling its earlier orders (test plan 14)

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 672 | GCC-12; DECISIONS (a store may be configured inactive; suspension must not break earlier obligations) | reviews: legal, owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** A store can be paused (browse-only, maintenance page or redirect to the default), switched off or reactivated without losing earlier orders: payments in flight finish; refunds, cancellations, tracking, support and webhooks still work; no product of the stopped store leaks into another.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** Nothing per country on main.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** #53 runbook and StorefrontKillSwitchTest: deactivating a country keeps its products out of every storefront and serves visitors the default; turning the flag off is not a kill switch (pinned). #53 a payment started in a switched-off or removed country still finishes there. #42 and #49 the sale gate refuses only new sales; refunds, cancellations, callbacks, due payments stay open (route lists pinned). #39 and lavkooapp#3, #5 sellable flag and browse-only notice.
- **(5) What remains.** No pause setting or modes (browse-only, maintenance, redirect) in the backend; the app's notice follows the legal sellable answer only. No end-to-end scenario test: place orders in a country, stop it, then refund, track, replay a webhook, answer support. The platform API is read-only and offers no pause. An order's country is derived from its lines, with no immutable snapshot.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#39](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/39) `8149090`; [lavkoo#42](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/42) `2534953`; [lavkoo#49](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/49) `b52d9b2`; [lavkoo#53](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/53) `7e32bba`; [lavkoo#83](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/83) `8ff3410`; [lavkooapp#3](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkooapp/pull/3) `eda7814`; [lavkooapp#5](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkooapp/pull/5) `5526c4b`; [lavkoo#111](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/111) `7e19d284`
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `ci` Deactivating keeps catalogues apart; turning the flag off is not a kill switch (tests/Feature/Storefront/StorefrontKillSwitchTest (SA, AE); PR 83 8ff3410: SQLite, Storefront MySQL 8, MariaDB 11.4.2)
  - `ci` An in-flight payment finishes after switch-off or removal; open-route list pinned (StorefrontPaymentCallbackTest, StorefrontSalesRoutesTest; same jobs)
  - `ci` App checkout gate and browse-only notice (lavkooapp#5 5526c4b and #7 56deea7: job storefront-header success (checkout_sale_gate_test, storefront_browse_only_notice_test))
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** The pause policy and its customer text are owner and legal inputs; Arabic text needs a native reader. Obligations after closure (refund windows) need the legal view.
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [MET] A deactivated country's products never show in another storefront and its visitor is served the default storefront (StorefrontKillSwitchTest; PR 83 jobs)
  - [MET] A payment started in a country switched off or removed since still builds its order there (StorefrontPaymentCallbackTest; PR 83 jobs)
  - [MET] Refunds, cancellations, callbacks and due payments on placed orders stay open for a storefront that may not sell (StorefrontSalesRoutesTest::test_refunds_cancellations_callbacks_and_obligations_on_placed_orders_stay_open; PR 83 jobs)
  - [MISSING] A per-store pause mode (browse-only, maintenance page, redirect) can be set and is honoured by web and apps (No mode in config/gcc.php or the resolver; app follows the sellable answer only)
  - [CODED] After a store is stopped, its earlier orders can be tracked, refunded, supported and their webhooks processed (end-to-end scenario) (PR 111 (7e19d284): StorefrontStopContinuityTest, 65 tests: ten driven tests in six cases each (Kuwait stopped browse only, in maintenance and by redirect; the default storefront stopped browse only; no stop on each of those two as the controls) and five route-table tests. In every driven case a new sale is refused when the storefront is stopped and taken in the controls. Tracked: the list, details, tracking and history of an order that the real checkout of the API placed before the stop, and the status of its shipment after a Torod webhook (the shipment row is inserted by the test and the worker step that applies the stored notice is called directly). Refunded: only the customer's refund request is driven, stored as pending; no refund decision and no payout. Supported: a reply to a support ticket opened before the stop and a chat message of the customer who placed the order, not tied to the order. Webhooks: the Torod webhook of that shipment; for MyFatoorah, a payment started before the stop is settled by the signed webhook after it and that webhook makes the order (no earlier order exists), and an order made by the webhook before the stop gets a repeated notice answered 'duplicate' and the customer's return redirected to payment-success; no order placed by the real checkout is paid by a webhook. Each driven test places its own order (or payment) and runs one action (the others are the cancellation, the due payment by cash and by transfer, and the earnings of a delivered order booked by both writers below the route), so there is no single end-to-end scenario on one order. The five route-table tests only pin that the staff side of a refund, the routes that mark an order delivered, the website's own order pages, the other gateways and couriers and the sales-channel webhooks, and the settlement routes carry neither stop gate. Not covered: a refund decision or payout, a courier submission, a settlement run, a storefront switched off in the configuration, any storefront other than Kuwait and the default one; the criterion says 'refunded' and 'end-to-end scenario', so it stays CODED. CI: run 355 (id 44412761889) on 7e19d284 (PR 111's own pull_request run, on the merge of the head into its base 01cefbc1, an ancestor of the head, so the tree is the head's), read per job: 'Tests, SQLite' on PHP 8.2 and on PHP 8.3 (the whole suite: 7,230 tests, 99 skipped, none of them in StorefrontStopContinuityTest) and 'Storefront tests' on MySQL 8 and on MariaDB 11.4.2 (941 tests, 4,660 assertions, none skipped): success, so all 65 tests ran on those four engines; 22 of the 23 jobs are success and the deployment overlay is red by design. The strict-mode MariaDB job is success but does not run tests/Feature/Storefront; with DB_STRICT_TESTS=1 the 60 driven cases skip until PR 89 and only the five route-table tests run, so no driven case has run in strict mode at this head.)
- **(10) Next coding task.** On #42/#53: config storefronts.countries.XX.mode (active, browse_only, maintenance, redirect) with a StorefrontPause middleware, and tests/Feature/Storefront/StorefrontStopScenarioTest: seed three orders, a pending MyFatoorah payment and a refund request in AE, set AE to each mode, assert new sales refused, old orders tracked, the refund decided, the webhook processed, the redirect target valid.
- **Open owner decisions.** The pause policy per mode and its customer text (native Arabic); whether POS keeps selling in a paused store; who may pause (staff scope, platform API level 2); how refund windows run after closure.

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 537-558, 598-600, 613 | Charter s.3 item 4 and s.4; DECISIONS guardrails 1-3 and 6; Discovery report 07; plan rules on new tables and historical relations | reviews: security, owner-decision

  - [CODED] Adding the country marker leaves every current_stock unchanged (The marker is one migration, database/migrations/2026_11_25_100000_add_storefront_country_to_products.php (PR 47, e0dd5dc2; the same blob on the 44 open heads that carry it): its up() adds one column with the default 'SA' and reads or writes no other, so no current_stock can change, and none of the 28 migration files on the 134 open pull request heads names current_stock or a stock table. CODED, not MET: no test holds a product row from before the marker and compares its stock afterwards. tests/Feature/Storefront/StorefrontCatalogTest.php (one blob on the 48 branches that carry it) runs the migration in setUp() before it inserts its products, and test_the_migration_run_again_changes_nothing compares only the country. The fingerprint that Discovery-01 report 07 section 3.5 step 1 asks for (before phase 1, after phases 1 and 2) exists on none of the 142 origin branches. Read-only reports that could take it are on origin/main: fulfillment:compatibility gives the units in stock and the products whose stock differs from their variant sum (CompatibilityCommandTest), costs:reconcile-platform compares the platform stock with the cost layers (PlatformReconciliationTest). M2-17 reconciles wallet, loyalty, order and payment totals only; PR 24 (eebb5884) and PR 18 (de1270f8) add tests only and PR 17 (5cbc3844) fixes the receipt of a return.)
  - [MET] Holds and the outbox reconcile against the movements that made them (`monitoring:reconcile-stock` (commit `5a1846de`) is the reconciler this line said no branch had: `App\Services\Monitoring\Reconciliation\StockHoldReconciler` compares each hold with the order or channel order that justifies it and each outbox row with its own record of what was done, and the command is built on the same read-only machinery as `wallet:reconcile` -- session locked read-only, every statement recorded, the report ending in the proof that none was a write -- and it changes nothing it finds, so it can be run on a restored copy by someone who may not change it. Exit 1 while anything is unexplained, so a backfill cannot start on a copy whose stock does not add up. Seven findings, each with its counterpart that must NOT be reported: a hold with nothing behind it; a hold whose order or channel order is GONE (a released hold is not reported: it holds nothing); a hold still `reserved` although its order is delivered, cancelled, returned or failed (the hold of an open order is not reported); a dead outbox row with no `failure_code` -- Discovery's own 'unexplained dead event' -- beside one with a code, which is not reported; a `worked` row with no `outcome`; and a claim older than six hours, which is a WARNING and does not block. A table that is not there is reported as NOT COMPARED, never as no findings. Tests: `tests/Feature/Monitoring/StockReconciliationTest` (12 tests, 60 assertions) on SQLite, on MariaDB 10.11.14 and on MariaDB in strict mode (`DB_STRICT_TESTS=1`). Teeth: the released-hold exclusion removed from 'holds whose order is gone' makes the count 2 instead of 1 and the test fails. **A stale claim this found and fixed (commit `760eef3a`):** `app/Services/Monitoring/Panels/InventoryIntegrityPanel.php` published `stuck_stock_reservations` as `not_supported`, source 'no table', with the note 'This build has no reservation of any kind' -- true when it was written, false since 2026-11-07, so the page told an operator there was nothing of the kind to look at. It now reads `inventory_reservations` (`state = reserved`, older than the sales-channel hub's own `aged_hold_days`, so page and hub cannot disagree) and keeps the honest `not_configured` answer where the table is absent. Teeth: the state filter widened to match everything makes the count 2 instead of 1 and the test fails.)
  - [MET] Provider stock reconciles against the platform's own balance (`App\Services\Monitoring\Reconciliation\ProviderStockReconciler` (commit `5a1846de`) makes exactly the comparison Discovery-01 report 07 section 3.5 step 3 describes: `provider_product_mappings.provider_stock` against `products.current_stock`, less what is promised. The comparison is deliberately ONE-SIDED, and that is not a weaker test: a store order takes units off the storefront the moment it is placed, so between two syncs the storefront is legitimately BELOW the provider's figure less what is promised, and reporting that direction would make the check noise -- it is INFO with a count. The other direction is a FAILURE: offering more than the provider has left is an oversell waiting for a customer, and on a shared shelf the units belong to another market. It asks `InventorySyncService::reservedNotYetWithProvider()` and `ReportsChannelHolds::unitsHeld()` for the promised units rather than restating the writer's equation, because a reconciler that restates it agrees with the version of the writer it was copied from; `ReadOnlyRunner` makes the inspected connection the default for the run, so those reads go to the database under inspection and the probe records them. What it proves nothing about is counted and named in the report, not hidden: a mapping whose product is assigned to another provider, a product sold per variant against one whole-product figure (which the writer also refuses), a provider code this build does not know, and the read being the first 2,000 mappings by id. Tests: `tests/Feature/Monitoring/StockReconciliationTest` (the four provider cases of 12) on SQLite, MariaDB 10.11.14 and MariaDB in strict mode. Teeth: the promised term replaced by 0 makes the case where an untaken order lowers what is left to offer fail, which is what proves the reuse of the writer is load-bearing. NOT covered by this criterion's wording and worth saying: `inventory_location_balances` is still compared with nothing -- this reconciler reads the provider's figure and the product's column, not the per-location figure.)
  - [CODED] Prices of existing products are unchanged by the marker (The marker migration of PR 47 (e0dd5dc2, database/migrations/2026_11_25_100000_add_storefront_country_to_products.php) adds one defaulted column and writes no other, none of the 28 migration files on the 134 open pull request heads names unit_price or purchase_price, and none of the 29 console files those heads add touches a product's price. CODED, not MET: no test compares the price of a product that existed before the marker with its price afterwards. The nearest test, StorefrontCatalogTest::test_each_country_keeps_its_own_price_and_stock_for_the_same_product (green in run 44178948609 on PR 83, 8ff34109), reads the price of rows inserted after the migration.)
- **(10) Next coding task.** See the criteria: the unmet ones name the file and the line that has to change.
